Mathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.OfFn

Polynomial.ofFndefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.OfFn, function or constant
Wording
the polynomial of n (from the name)
English
Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the polynomial of $n$ is a linear map of the set of functions from elements of $\mathrm{Fin}$ applied to $n$ to elements of $R$ and the set of polynomials of $R$.
Formula
$n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{ofFn} (n)\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{Fin} (n)\rightarrow R , \mathrm{Polynomial} (R))$
French
Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Alors le polynôme de $n$ est une application linéaire de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $\mathrm{Fin}$ appliqué à $n$ à des éléments de $R$ et l'ensemble de polynômes de $R$.
Polynomial.toFndefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.OfFn, function or constant
Wording
the function of n (from the name)
English
Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the function of $n$ is a linear map of the set of polynomials of $R$ and the set of functions from elements of $\mathrm{Fin}$ applied to $n$ to elements of $R$ defined as the product of the function that maps $i$ to $\operatorname{lcoeff}$ applied to $R$ and $\operatorname{val}$ applied to $i$.
Formula
$n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{toFn} (n):= \operatorname{pi} (\lambda i . \operatorname{lcoeff} (R , \operatorname{val} (i)))\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{Polynomial} (R), \mathrm{Fin} (n)\rightarrow R)$
French
Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Alors la fonction de $n$ est une application linéaire de l'ensemble de polynômes de $R$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $\mathrm{Fin}$ appliqué à $n$ à des éléments de $R$ défini comme le produit de la fonction qui envoie $i$ à $\operatorname{lcoeff}$ appliqué à $R$ et $\operatorname{val}$ appliqué à $i$.

Mathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Reverse

Polynomial.reflectdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Reverse, function or constant
Wording
the reflection of N and x1 (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $N$ be a natural number. Let $x_{1}$ be a polynomial of $R$. Then the reflection of $N$ and $x_{1}$ is a polynomial of $R$.
Formula
$N \in \Natural , x_{1} \in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)\vdash \operatorname{reflect} (N , x_{1})\in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)$
French
Soit $N$ un nombre naturel. Soit $x_{1}$ un polynôme de $R$. Alors la réflexion de $N$ et $x_{1}$ est un polynôme de $R$.

Polynomial.reversedefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Reverse, function or constant
Wording
the reverse of f (from the docstring)
English
Let $f$ be a polynomial of $R$. Then the reverse of $f$ is a polynomial of $R$ defined as the reflection of the natural degree of $f$ and $f$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)\vdash \operatorname{reverse} (f):= \operatorname{reflect} (\operatorname{natDegree} (f), f)\in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)$
French
Soit $f$ un polynôme de $R$. Alors le renversement de $f$ est un polynôme de $R$ défini comme la réflexion du degré naturel de $f$ et $f$.

Mathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Roots

Polynomial.arootsdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Roots, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be a polynomial of $T$. Let $S$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{aroots}$ applied to $p$ and $S$ is a multiset of $S$ defined as the root of the map of the algebra map of $T$ and $S$ and $p$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{Polynomial} (T), S \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{aroots} (p , S):= \operatorname{roots} (\operatorname{map} (\operatorname{algebraMap} (T , S), p))\in \mathrm{Multiset} (S)$
French
Soit $p$ un polynôme de $T$. Soit $S$ un ensemble. Alors $\operatorname{aroots}$ appliqué à $p$ et $S$ est un multiensemble de $S$ défini comme la racine de l'application de l'application d'algèbres de $T$ et $S$ et $p$.
Polynomial.nthRootsdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Roots, function or constant
Wording
the nth root of n and a (from the name)
English
Let $n$ be a natural number. Let $a$ be an element of $R$. Then the nth root of $n$ and $a$ is a multiset of $R$ defined as the root of $\operatorname{X} ^ {n}- \operatorname{C} (a)$.
Formula
$n \in \Natural , a \in R \vdash \operatorname{nthRoots} (n , a):= \operatorname{roots} (\operatorname{X} ^ {n}- \operatorname{C} (a))\in \mathrm{Multiset} (R)$
French
Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Soit $a$ un élément de $R$. Alors la racine nième de $n$ et $a$ est un multiensemble de $R$ défini comme la racine de $\operatorname{X} ^ {n}- \operatorname{C} (a)$.

Formula
$n \in \Natural , a \in R \vdash \operatorname{nthRootsFinset} (n , a):= \operatorname{toFinset} (\operatorname{nthRoots} (n , a))\in \mathrm{Finset} (R)$

English
Let $p$ be a polynomial of $T$. Let $S$ be a set. Then the root set of $p$ and $S$ is an element of $\wp S$ defined as the finite set of $\operatorname{aroots}$ applied to $p$ and $S$.

Polynomial.Sequence.basisdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.Sequence, function or constant
Wording
the basis of S and hCoeff (from the name)
English
Let $S$ be a sequence of $R$ such that for all natural numbers $i$, the leading coefficient of $\operatorname{elems'}$ applied to $S$ and $i$ is a unit. Then the basis of $S$ and $\mathrm{hCoeff}$ is a basis of $R$ and the set of polynomials of $R$.
Formula
$S \in \operatorname{Sequence} (R), \forall i \in \Natural . \mathrm{IsUnit} (\operatorname{leadingCoeff} (\operatorname{elems'} (S , i)))\vdash \operatorname{basis} (S , \mathrm{hCoeff})\in \operatorname{Basis} (\Natural , R , \mathrm{Polynomial} (R))$
French
Soit $S$ une suite de $R$ telle que pour tous les nombres naturels $i$, le coefficient dominant de $\operatorname{elems'}$ appliqué à $S$ et $i$ soit une unité. Alors la base de $S$ et $\mathrm{hCoeff}$ est une base de $R$ et l'ensemble de polynômes de $R$.

Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a polynomial of $R$. Then $\operatorname{Splits}$ holds for $f$, if $f$ is an element of the closure of $\UnionunionMACRoAB {\operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda x_{1} . \mathrm{exists} (R , \lambda a . \EqtMACRoH {\operatorname{C} (a)}{x_{1}}))}{\operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda x_{2} . \mathrm{exists} (R , \lambda a_{1} . \EqtMACRoH {(\operatorname{X} + \operatorname{C} (a_{1}))}{x_{2}}))}$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)\vdash \operatorname{Splits} (f):\Leftrightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{closure} (\UnionunionMACRoAI {\operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda x_{1} . \mathrm{exists} (R , \lambda a . \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{C} (a)}{x_{1}}))}{\operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda x_{2} . \mathrm{exists} (R , \lambda a_{1} . \EqtMACRoJ {(\operatorname{X} + \operatorname{C} (a_{1}))}{x_{2}}))})}{f}$

Mathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.UnitTrinomial

Polynomial.IsUnitTrinomialdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.UnitTrinomial, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be a polynomial of $\Integer$. Then $\operatorname{IsUnitTrinomial}$ holds for $p$, if there exist elements $k$, $m$ and $n$ of $\Natural$, such that there exists an element $h$ of $\mathrm{Lt} (k , m)$, such that there exists an element $h_{1}$ of $\mathrm{Lt} (m , n)$, such that there exist units $u$, $v$ and $w$ of $\Integer$, such that $p$ is equal to $\operatorname{trinomial}$ applied to $k$, $m$, $n$, the underlying value in the base of $u$, the underlying value in the base of $v$ and the underlying value in the base of $w$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{Polynomial} (\Integer)\vdash \operatorname{IsUnitTrinomial} (p):\Leftrightarrow \exists k \in \Natural . \exists m \in \Natural . \exists n \in \Natural . \exists h \in \mathrm{Lt} (k , m). \exists h_{1} \in \mathrm{Lt} (m , n). \exists u \in \UnitsMACRoAX {\Integer}. \exists v \in \UnitsMACRoAX {\Integer}. \exists w \in \UnitsMACRoAX {\Integer}. \EqtMACRoJ {p}{\operatorname{trinomial} (k , m , n , \operatorname{val} (u), \operatorname{val} (v), \operatorname{val} (w))}$
French
Soit $p$ un polynôme de $\Integer$. Alors $\operatorname{IsUnitTrinomial}$ tient pour $p$ s'il existent des éléments $k$, $m$ et $n$ de $\Natural$ tel que il existe un élément $h$ de $\mathrm{Lt} (k , m)$ tel que il existe un élément $h_{1}$ de $\mathrm{Lt} (m , n)$ tel que il existent des unités $u$, $v$ et $w$ de $\Integer$ tel que $p$ soit égal à $\operatorname{trinomial}$ appliqué à $k$, $m$, $n$, le [DepNoun] sous-jacent de $u$, le [DepNoun] sous-jacent de $v$ et le [DepNoun] sous-jacent de $w$.
Polynomial.trinomialdefMathlib.Algebra.Polynomial.UnitTrinomial, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $k$, $m$ and $n$ be natural numbers. Let $u$, $v$ and $w$ be elements of $R$. Then $\operatorname{trinomial}$ applied to $k$, $m$, $n$, $u$, $v$ and $w$ is a polynomial of $R$ defined as $\operatorname{C} (u)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {k}+ \operatorname{C} (v)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {m}+ \operatorname{C} (w)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {n}$.
Formula
$k , m , n \in \Natural , u , v , w \in R \vdash \operatorname{trinomial} (k , m , n , u , v , w):= \operatorname{C} (u)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {k}+ \operatorname{C} (v)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {m}+ \operatorname{C} (w)\times \operatorname{X} ^ {n}\in \mathrm{Polynomial} (R)$
French

Mathlib.Algebra.Prime.Defs

PrimedefMathlib.Algebra.Prime.Defs, predicate
Wording
p is prime (by hand)
English
Let $p$ be an element of $M$. Then $p$ is prime, if $p \neq 0$, $p$ is not a unit and for all elements $a$ of $M$, for all elements $b$ of $M$, if $p$ divides $a \times b$, then $p$ divides $a$ or $p$ divides $b$.
Formula
$p \in M \vdash \mathrm{Prime} (p):\Leftrightarrow p \neq 0 \wedge (\neg \mathrm{IsUnit} (p)\wedge (\forall a \in M . \forall b \in M . \divides {p}{a \times b}\rightarrow \divides {p}{a}\vee \divides {p}{b}))$
French
Soit $p$ un élément de $M$. Alors $p$ est premier si $p \neq 0$, $p$ n'est pas une unité et pour tous des éléments $a$ de $M$, pour tous des éléments $b$ de $M$, si $p$ divise $a \times b$, alors $p$ divise $a$ ou $p$ divise $b$.

RingEquiv.ofNonUnitalRingHomdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Equiv, function or constant
Wording
the ring isomorphism of hom and inv (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{hom}$ be a non unital ring homomorphism of $R$ and $S$. Let $\mathrm{inv}$ be a non unital ring homomorphism of $S$ and $R$ such that the composition of $\mathrm{inv}$ and $\mathrm{hom}$ is equal to the identity of $R$ and the composition of $\mathrm{hom}$ and $\mathrm{inv}$ is equal to the identity of $S$. French

Alors l'isomorphisme d'anneaux de $\mathrm{hom}$ et $\mathrm{inv}$ est un isomorphisme d'anneaux de $R$ et $S$.
RingEquiv.ofRingHomdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Equiv, function or constant
Wording
the ring isomorphism of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a ring homomorphism of $R$ and $S$. Let $g$ be a ring homomorphism of $S$ and $R$ such that the composition of $f$ and $g$ is equal to the identity ring homomorphism of $S$ and the composition of $g$ and $f$ is equal to the identity ring homomorphism of $R$. Then the ring isomorphism of $f$ and $g$ is a ring isomorphism of $R$ and $S$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{RingHom} (R , S), g \in \mathrm{RingHom} (S , R), \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{comp} (f , g)}{\operatorname{id} (S)}, \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{comp} (g , f)}{\operatorname{id} (R)}\vdash \operatorname{ofRingHom} (f , g , h , h_{1})\in \mathrm{RingEquiv} (R , S)$
French

Soit $g$ un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $S$ et $R$ tel que la composition de $f$ et $g$ soit égale à l'homomorphisme d'anneaux d'identités de $S$ et la composition de $g$ et $f$ soit égale à l'homomorphisme d'anneaux d'identités de $R$. Alors l'isomorphisme d'anneaux de $f$ et $g$ est un isomorphisme d'anneaux de $R$ et $S$.

RingEquiv.opdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Equiv, function or constant

the opposite (from the name)

English
Let $f$ be a ring homomorphism of $R$ and $S$. Let $g$ be a function from elements of $S$ to elements of $R$ such that $\operatorname{LeftInverse}$ holds for $g$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$ and $\operatorname{RightInverse}$ holds for $g$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$. Then the inverse of $f$ and $g$ is a ring homomorphism of $S$ and $R$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{RingHom} (R , S), g \in S \rightarrow R , \operatorname{LeftInverse} (g , \operatorname{coe} (f)), \operatorname{RightInverse} (g , \operatorname{coe} (f))\vdash \operatorname{inverse} (f , g , h , h_{1})\in \mathrm{RingHom} (S , R)$
French
Soit $f$ un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $R$ et $S$. Soit $g$ une fonction des éléments de $S$ à des éléments de $R$ telle que $\operatorname{LeftInverse}$ tienne pour $g$ et $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$ et $\operatorname{RightInverse}$ tienne pour $g$ et $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$. Alors l'inverse de $f$ et $g$ est un homomorphisme d'anneaux de $S$ et $R$.

$f \in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (R , S), \forall x_{2} \in R . \forall y \in R . \mathrm{Commute} (f (x_{2}), f (y))\vdash \operatorname{fromOpposite} (f , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (\mathrm{MulOpposite} (R), S)$
French
Soit $f$ un homomorphisme d'anneaux non unitaire de $R$ et $S$ tel que pour tous les éléments $x_{2}$ et $y$ de $R$, $\mathrm{Commute}$ tienne pour $f$ appliqué à $x_{2}$ et $f$ appliqué à $y$. Alors $\operatorname{fromOpposite}$ appliqué à $f$ et $\mathrm{hf}$ est un homomorphisme d'anneaux non unitaire de l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $R$ et $S$.
NonUnitalRingHom.opdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Opposite, function or constant
Wording
the opposite (from the name)
English
The opposite is an equivalence from the set of non unital ring homomorphisms of $R$ and $S$ to the set of non unital ring homomorphisms of the set of multiplicative opposites of $R$ and the set of multiplicative opposites of $S$.
Formula
$\operatorname{op} \in \EquivMACRoAL {\mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (R , S)}{\mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (\mathrm{MulOpposite} (R), \mathrm{MulOpposite} (S))}$
French
L'opposé est une équivalence de l'ensemble d'homomorphismes d'anneaux non unitaires de $R$ et $S$ à l'ensemble d'homomorphismes d'anneaux non unitaires de l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $R$ et l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $S$.
NonUnitalRingHom.toOppositedefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Opposite, function or constant
Wording
the opposite of f and hf (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a non unital ring homomorphism of $R$ and $S$ such that for all elements $x_{2}$ and $y$ of $R$, $\mathrm{Commute}$ holds for $f$ applied to $x_{2}$ and $f$ applied to $y$. Then the opposite of $f$ and $\mathrm{hf}$ is a non unital ring homomorphism of $R$ and the set of multiplicative opposites of $S$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (R , S), \forall x_{2} \in R . \forall y \in R . \mathrm{Commute} (f (x_{2}), f (y))\vdash \operatorname{toOpposite} (f , \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (R , \mathrm{MulOpposite} (S))$
French

NonUnitalRingHom.unopdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Opposite, function or constant
Wording
the opposite (from the name)
English

Formula
$\operatorname{unop} := \operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{op})\in \EquivMACRoAL {\mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (\mathrm{MulOpposite} (R), \mathrm{MulOpposite} (S))}{\mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (R , S)}$

Mathlib.Algebra.Ring.Parity

OdddefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Parity, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $a$ be an element of $x$. Then $\mathrm{Odd}$ holds for $a$, if there exists an element $k$ of $x$, such that $\EqtMACRoH {a}{2 \times k + 1}$.
Formula
$a \in x \vdash \mathrm{Odd} (a):\Leftrightarrow \exists k \in x . \EqtMACRoJ {a}{2 \times k + 1}$
French
Soit $a$ un élément de $x$. Alors $\mathrm{Odd}$ tient pour $a$ s'il existe un élément $k$ de $x$ tel que $\EqtMACRoH {a}{2 \times k + 1}$.

Mathlib.Algebra.Ring.Periodic

Function.AntiperiodicdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Periodic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$. Let $c$ be an element of $x$. Then $\operatorname{Antiperiodic}$ holds for $f$ and $c$, if for all elements $x_{4}$ of $x$, $\EqtMACRoH {f (x_{4} + c)}{\operatorname{neg} (f (x_{4}))}$.
Formula
$f \in x \rightarrow x_{1} , c \in x \vdash \operatorname{Antiperiodic} (f , c):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{4} \in x . \EqtMACRoJ {f (x_{4} + c)}{\operatorname{neg} (f (x_{4}))}$
French

the lift of h and x3 (from the name)

Mathlib.Algebra.Ring.Pi

NonUnitalRingHom.compLeftdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Pi, function or constant
Wording
the left composition of f and I (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a non unital ring homomorphism of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $I$ be a set. Then the left composition of $f$ and $I$ is a non unital ring homomorphism of the set of functions from elements of $I$ to elements of $x$ and the set of functions from elements of $I$ to elements of $x_{1}$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (x , x_{1}), I \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{compLeft} (f , I)\in \mathrm{NonUnitalRingHom} (I \rightarrow x , I \rightarrow x_{1})$
French

Alors la composition gauche de $f$ et $I$ est un homomorphisme d'anneaux non unitaire de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $I$ à des éléments de $x$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $I$ à des éléments de $x_{1}$.

NonUnitalRingHom.prodMapdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Prod, function or constant
Wording
the product map of f and g (from the name)
English

Then the product map of $f$ and $g$ is a non unital ring homomorphism of $\mathrm{Prod}$ applied to $R$ and $S$ and $\mathrm{Prod}$ applied to $\mathrm{R'}$ and $\mathrm{S'}$ defined as the product of the composition of $f$ and the first component of $R$ and $S$ and the composition of $g$ and the second component of $R$ and $S$.

French
L'équivalence opposée est un isomorphisme d'ordres de l'ensemble de sous-semi-anneaux de $R$ et l'ensemble de sous-semi-anneaux de l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $R$ défini comme $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{op}$, $\operatorname{unop}$, $\mathrm{Subsemiring\_\_unop\_op}$ et $\mathrm{Subsemiring\_\_op\_unop}$ et la fonction qui envoie $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$ à $\mathrm{Subsemiring\_\_op\_le\_op\_iff}$.
Subsemiring.ringEquivOpMopdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Subsemiring.MulOpposite, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $S$ be a subsemiring of $R$. Then $\operatorname{ringEquivOpMop}$ applied to $S$ is a ring isomorphism of $\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {S}{x_{1}})}$ and the set of multiplicative opposites of $\SubtypeSet {x_{2}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{op} (S)}{x_{2}})}$.
Formula
$S \in \mathrm{Subsemiring} (R)\vdash \operatorname{ringEquivOpMop} (S)\in \mathrm{RingEquiv} (\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {S}{x_{1}})}, \mathrm{MulOpposite} (\SubtypeSet {x_{2}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{op} (S)}{x_{2}})}))$
French
Soit $S$ un sous-semi-anneau de $R$. Alors $\operatorname{ringEquivOpMop}$ appliqué à $S$ est un isomorphisme d'anneaux de $\SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {S}{x_{1}})}$ et l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $\SubtypeSet {x_{2}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {\operatorname{op} (S)}{x_{2}})}$.
Subsemiring.unopdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Subsemiring.MulOpposite, function or constant
Wording
the opposite of S (from the name)
English
Let $S$ be a subsemiring of the set of multiplicative opposites of $R$. Then the opposite of $S$ is a subsemiring of $R$.
Formula
$S \in \mathrm{Subsemiring} (\mathrm{MulOpposite} (R))\vdash \operatorname{unop} (S)\in \mathrm{Subsemiring} (R)$
French
Soit $S$ un sous-semi-anneau de l'ensemble d'opposés multiplicatifs de $R$. Alors l'opposé de $S$ est un sous-semi-anneau de $R$.

Mathlib.Algebra.Ring.Subsemiring.Order

Subsemiring.nonnegdefMathlib.Algebra.Ring.Subsemiring.Order, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{nonneg}$ applied to $R$ is a subsemiring of $R$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{nonneg} (R)\in \mathrm{Subsemiring} (R)$
French
Soit $R$ un ensemble. Alors $\operatorname{nonneg}$ appliqué à $R$ est un sous-semi-anneau de $R$.
